CRASH LAB / PAELEO / PAE-02

PAE-02 · one description, many operations

The threshold, handmade

Hold the description and the dog's choices still. Let each swarm build everything else from the bottom up. Do the choices survive, and is each world its own?

PAE-01 showed every SCION swarm as a lens on one paper dog: a style laid over a drawing it did not make. That shows what each swarm would look at, not what it would make. In PAE-02 each swarm builds the same scene itself: what the dog is made of, how it moves, what the place is and what it sounds like. Only two things are shared: the description and the score of the dog's six choices from the PAELEO machine.

The swarms The brief The write-up

Held still, then let go
P
the prompts: call, call and reach, sit down beside the door, open the door, offer a hand low, say it softly, come in
E
the ekphrasis and the score: six choices at fixed times, 768 frames
O
the operation: entirely the swarm's

If description is operative, the choices survive every operation, and that can be measured. Whether they read as choices is for a critic to say.

The swarms so far

54 of the 54, in cohorts chosen to be as far apart as possible in what they build with. Each later cohort reads what the earlier ones failed at, never their films or code.

The invariants are the description (the six choices within ¼ s, never inside the house and ending on the sill, moving with the score, the back shown in the refusal). Everything that differs between the films is the operation.

SCION 01Cut-out collage · 38 s · PASS

Terry Gilliam

Each choice is a substitution, so the dog's decision is also the film's cut.

Made of

The real archive dog from a 1930s home movie, cut out by its mask and engraved by hatching its own light; card parts on pins, a tail cut from its own back fur. Every other figure is a found public-domain cut-out, engraved the same way.

Moved by

Card pivots, held frames and hard substitution. The call turns the woman's head into a hinged shouting mouth and the dog's head into a door; the refusal is the card turned over to its blank back, stamped NO; the approach is earned by the door swapped for a lit slab; the lying is a lying card.

The sheets

Did the description survive?

Measured from this swarm's own render (its trace) by check_handmade.py. The score's time, then the time the choice appears.

backs away 1.00 → 1.00 swatches 2.33 → 2.33 sturns its back 3.42 → 3.42 swatches 7.46 → 7.46 scomes, head low 14.71 → 14.71 slies across the sill 23.79 → 23.79 s
  • Never inside. Holds: closest 0.59 m from the door (the sill is at 0.56); ends 0.59 m.
  • Follows the score. Holds: r = 0.944 between its position and the score's.
  • The refusal shows the back. Holds: turned in 98% of the refusal's frames.

The critics

Each round is a fresh agent that sees the film, the brief and this swarm's SCION prompt, never the builder's notes, and returns a verdict and falsifiable repairs. Stopped at an independent pass.

Critic round 1 · PASS (narrow)
# SCION 01 · critic round 1 · PASS (narrow)

Independent critic (fresh agent; 129 scene frames plus full-resolution stills; verdict from the picture before any builder notes).

Per choice: backing away weak (the door-head gag hides the crouch and lowered head); watch reads (2.3); turned back reads (the card's blank back faces away, 3.6; lies with back turned 4.6–7.5); turning to look reads (7.6–8.0, facing the open door); coming head low reads (14.6–23.5); lying across the sill reads (front half inside the frame, rear and tail out); looking in weak (head lifts at 26.7 but points at the jamb, not into the light), staying reads.

Bottom-up: yes. Every part cut from found footage and engraved by luminance hatching; visible card pivots; state changes as hard substitutions (door-head, card verso, lying card); commands as Gilliam gags (hinged mouth, telescoping arm). Fundamentally not the PAE-00 cut-out.

Craft: the person faces away throughout; edge-on flip frames read as a stick creature; the neck card is an obvious rectangle in the close shot; the butterfly causes nothing (visual noise); the NO stamp is a caption; the cut-in at 25.4 is unmotivated.

Rights: all 7 found sources public domain, each matching found-frames.json; sound synthesized; end card credits every source.

Invariants hold on screen. Repair conditions (ranked):
1. t=26.6–27.6: head points up and into the lit door opening.
2. t=1.0–2.3: lowered head and crouch visible ≥0.5 s while backing, or the door-head sits visibly low.
3. t=3.5 and 7.6: the edge-on card reads as a card (thin edge, held frame).
4. t=0–4.6: the person or the mouth card faces the dog when calling.
5. t=7.8–10.5: the butterfly causes a visible reaction, or is cut.

Keep: the card verso as the turned back, the telescoping arm, the lit-door substitution, the lying card across the sill.
Critic round 2 · PASS
# SCION 01 · critic round 2 · PASS

Fresh independent critic (135 scene frames plus frame-level views of both flips; judged the picture before reading round 1).

All six choices read at their times: backing away (just: the door-head sits low, legs bent), turned back (edge held 2 frames, blank back stamped NO, lies with back turned to 7.4), turning to look (7.625, facing the lit door), coming head low (14.7–23.6), lying across the sill (front half in the light, rear and tail out), looking in and staying (head rises into the opening 26.7–28.0, clearest 27.2; competes with the moth).

Bottom-up: engraved luminance-hatched cut-outs with visible paper edges; every state change a hard substitution (door-head, card back, lying card, lit-door slab, hinged mouth card, telescoping arm).

Round-1 conditions: all five met (2 marginally; 3 with one messy frame at 7.583).

Remaining defects (optional polish): moth above the head during the look-in; no sound on the approach start and the lie-down sound ~0.3 s late; crumpled frame at 7.583; the low hand reads as a dropped glove; the person's body faces away; the cut-in at 25.4 unmotivated.

Verdict: PASS against the brief and the SCION bar. Stopped here per the SCION doctrine (stop at an independent pass); the polish list is kept for a later round.

SCION 02Silhouette theatre · 38 s · PASS

Lotte Reiniger

No colour on any figure: the whole relationship has to read by outline and the gap between shapes.

Made of

A hand-cut shepherd silhouette of 23 pieces on 22 pins, its proportions measured from the archive dog's pose keys (not traced). Tissue hills, a card house front, black card with holes the lamp shines through, lace-cut ornament.

Moved by

Articulated joints on a lit screen, paws planted by a contact solver; turns are made by swapping in cut end-on pieces (a rear view, a front view) and four interchangeable heads, and the head always leads the body.

The sheets

Reads as a dog in every profile; fails for 1–3 frames in each big turn (3.97, 7.63: the flat puppet squashed sideways into an arched shape).

Bottom-up: pure black silhouettes on a lamp-lit screen, translucent planes behind, hinged pieces with round lap joints, solved legs with planted paws, end-on replacement pieces, lace-cut ornament. Fundamentally different from PAE-00 (no colour on the figures; outline and gaps only; light from behind; different animal and place). Not a restyle.

Craft: squashed in-betweens at 3.97 and 7.63 (joint limits logged, not enforced); door already ajar and lit at 0 s; sill not a separate surface; ambiguous look-in; "Come in." half hidden in the plaque 26–28. Sound follows events; commands loudest, soft words quieter.

Rights: clean (original, public domain, synthesized).

Verdict: PASS. Repair conditions (ranked):

2. Door leaf visibly closed before 6.2 s, swinging open 6.2–7.0, light floods only then.
3. Look-in at 26.4: snout into the doorway's light, nose overlapping the arch, eye hole hidden.
4. A raised sill piece separate from the porch floor, the dog's chest on it from 24.4.
5. "Come in." fully inside the plaque on every frame.

All six choices read, each within ~0.15 s of the score: backing away (crouch, head low, steps back facing the door); turned back (profile, then a cut rear-view piece 4.01–4.30, lies facing away 4.34–7.4); turning to look (lifts the head 7.57, over the shoulder, a face-on piece 8.03–8.32, profile to the door from 8.36: "good substitution acting"); coming head low (even pace from 14.77); lying across the sill (forequarters on the raised sill inside the lit arch, haunches and tail out); looking in and staying (muzzle into the light, eye hidden, 27.0–27.7; chin back on paws by 28.3). Reads as a dog in every frame checked, both turns included; no folded in-betweens.

Bottom-up: pure black hinged silhouettes on a lamp-lit screen, lace cut-outs, end-on replacement pieces, planted paws, tissue layers; relationships read without colour. Not a restyle of PAE-00. Rights clean.

Round-1 conditions: all five met.

Remaining polish (not pursued): the rear-view piece lacks a leg gap and hock (could read as a post); one frame at 7.57 joins a face-on head to a lying body; the door light turns the whole sky gold instead of spilling onto the porch; the 7.4 window is loud under the soft voice.

Verdict: PASS. Stopped here per the SCION doctrine (stop at an independent pass).

method · every asset and its rights · ledger of attempts · build · the prompt

SCION 03Surreal collage grammar · 38 s · PASS

Max Ernst

Authority becomes architecture, then the dog's own fur.

Made of

One plate of a collage-novel: a porch at dusk cut in wood-engraving line by one burin, so the seams hide. The person's commanding head is a lion cut from Andy's Animal Alphabet; the hand from The Door to Heaven; a miner's lamp from Anthracite. The dog is a solid maquette of 27 cones and ellipsoids, engraved along its form; no picture of a dog is used.

Moved by

The dog turns as a solid in the plate's camera and walks on a step solver. The collage moves by changing job: the lion's head leaves the person and becomes the keystone, whose knock opens the door; the same lion's mane is pasted on the dog with its white cut edge at the first call, and sinks into its coat, edge lost, as it lies across the sill. The grabbing hand returns small and low.

The sheets

Did the description survive?

  • Never inside. Holds: closest 0.59 m from the door (the sill is at 0.56); ends 0.59 m.
  • Follows the score. Holds: r = 1.000 between its position and the score's.
  • The refusal shows the back. Holds: turned in 94% of the refusal's frames.

Critic round 1 · PASS (narrow)
# SCION 03 · critic round 1 · PASS (narrow)

Independent critic (fresh agent; frames every 0.5 s plus dense windows, full resolution; verdict from the picture and sound before any builder notes).

Per choice: backing away reads (crouch, tail tucked, backs away facing the door); turned back reads (true 3D rotation through a frontal view that still reads as a dog, away by 3.79, lies turned away 4.4–7.45; at small scale a striped lump); turning to look reads (the head comes round to the door by 7.58–7.71, before the body); coming head low weak (the muzzle below the back, the top of the skull about level with it); lying across the sill reads (flat by 24.4, front half inside the door opening, tail out; a 2D overlap more than a crossing); looking in and staying reads (head into depth toward the lit hall by 26.63; settles and stays). A dog-like quadruped throughout, but the head is rodent-like crouching and pig- or otter-like lying.

Language: the lion-headed magistrate is a hybrid noun cut from a found engraving source, its white margin shown while it commands; at 5.5–6.0 the head leaves the man and becomes the keystone over the door, which then opens under it: one image causing the next, authority becoming architecture. The pasted hand changes job from grabbing to offered low. The lion serves the scene. But the dog, the person and the set are self-built 3D models with a hatching shader: a style over the swarm's own rig, not collage. The collage grammar stops at the dog. Fundamentally different from PAE-00.

Defects: the "it's all right" caption stays on screen 7.0–25.8, long after the voice; the person lurches to the chair; the door swings with no agent; feet slightly above the shadow at 3.67. Sound follows events; soft voice ≥12 dB under the calls. Rights clean (lion, hand and lantern match found-frames.json).

Repair conditions (ranked):
1. At 16, 19 and 22 s the top of the skull sits visibly below the back line.
2. The head reads as canine (longer muzzle, larger defined ears) at 1.9, 6.0 and 29.5 s.
3. At least one dog part carries the swarm's grammar: a found engraved fragment with a visible or concealed seam that changes job across the scene.
4. No caption on screen while no voice sounds (11.6–25.8).
5. The door's opening at 6.2–6.5 is visibly caused by something in the image.
Critic round 2 · PASS
# SCION 03 · critic round 2 · PASS

Fresh independent critic (all 768 scene frames, contact and crop sheets; judged the picture and sound before reading round 1).

All six choices read: backing away (crouches facing the door, moves away; the mane flares at 1.04); turned back (a real turn through a frontal view, every frame a dog; lies curled away 4.5–7.45); turning to look (head round to the door by 7.71; a brief tangle at 7.62–7.67); coming head low (the skull top below the back at 16, 19 and 22; feet plant and step); lying across the sill (flat by 24.3, head and chest inside the jambs, haunch and tail out; a side-on overlap); looking in and staying (the head turns away into the lit hall 26.6–27.9, settles, stays). The head mostly a dog's.

Collage grammar now reaches the dog. The lion from Andy's Animal Alphabet recurs in four changed jobs: the magistrate's head; the keystone over the door, whose knock sets the door swinging; the dog's raised hackles from the first call, white cut edge shown; the same mane shrunk, its edge hidden, as the dog lies down. One image causes the next, and authority ends up as the dog's own fur. The change reads at the lie-down, weakly during the approach. The dog's body is still the swarm's own model with engraved shading: a limit, no longer disqualifying.

Round-1 conditions: 1 and 3 met; 2 mostly met; 4 nearly met (the caption lingers to 12.29); 5 weakly met (small impact marks at the keystone).

Remaining (optional, not pursued: stopped at an independent pass per the SCION doctrine): the mane should visibly lie down or lose its edge during the approach, so trust shows before the sill; a stray hatching band in the light patch 24–31; the caption gone by 11.2; a readable silhouette 7.60–7.70. Soft voice ~10 dB under the calls, above room tone. Rights clean; end card true.

SCION 04Image torrent · 38 s · PASS (narrow)

Frank Mouris

The voice is made of pictures too, and it hits the dog as a ring of alarm clocks.

Made of

Thousands of tiles cut from 55 public-domain archive shots (2,238 frames), each figure a computed shape whose edge is the tiles themselves, down to 4×3 pixels, with no line drawn; the tiles' subjects mean something: the dog is animals and the archive dog, the person faces and hats, the door doors and ladders, the light lanterns and bulbs, the words alarm clocks and trumpets for COME HERE, flowers and swans for it's all right.

Moved by

The torrent's speed is the dog's state: frantic under the calls, every tile of the dog replaced at each choice, settling as it lies down, and still holds where each tile only plays its own shot forward.

The sheets

Did the description survive?

Measured from this swarm's own render (its trace) by check_handmade.py. The score's time, then the time the choice appears.

  • Never inside. Holds: closest 0.59 m from the door (the sill is at 0.56); ends 0.59 m.
  • Follows the score. Holds: r = 1.000 between its position and the score's.
  • The refusal shows the back. Holds: turned in 82% of the refusal's frames.

The critics

Each round is a fresh agent that sees the film, the brief and this swarm's SCION prompt, never the builder's notes, and returns a verdict and falsifiable repairs. Stopped at an independent pass.

Critic round 1 · PASS (narrow)
# SCION 04 · critic round 1 · PASS (narrow)

Independent critic (fresh agent; frames every 0.5 s plus dense windows, full resolution, frame-to-frame difference in a dog crop; verdict from the picture before any builder notes).

Per choice: backing away reads (crouch, head low, moves away; the wall tiles turn to trumpeters and alarms under the call); turned back reads (end-on box at 3.6–4.2, lies facing away); turning to look reads (rises ~7.5, facing flips 8.25–8.5 with the score's turn; end-on frame like a giraffe); coming head low weak (head near level; an unmotivated change from brown to bright orange cartoon fill at ~17–20); lying across the sill reads; looking in weak (reads as "looks up", not into the door). Reads as a dog throughout.

Boil: mild (mean frame difference 6–7/255 in a dog crop). Legible, not noise; too calm for Mouris.

Bottom-up: partly. Every surface is a quadtree of real archive tiles with meaningful subjects (animals for the dog, bulbs for the door, household objects for the wall, which switch with the words). But the dog's shape is a parametric silhouette with a drawn outline and eye; the tiles are a fill tinted until the images mostly disappear; the end card's "no figure is drawn" is not quite true; few rhythmic slots, holds or changes of cut speed. Different from PAE-00 in material, not in staging.

Craft: end-on views read as a table or a giraffe; unmotivated colour change; flat pink hand; dog tiles unreadable at playback size; tiny title line. Sound follows events.

Rights: 55 public domain, self-made, OFL fonts, one CC BY 4.0 entry; all clip_ids in found-frames.json.

Repair conditions (ranked):
1. 14.7–23.8: head clearly below the shoulder line (frames every 1 s).
2. 26.6–27.5: the head turns toward or into the lit doorway, not only upward.
3. At least two of the dog's tiles recognisable as archive images at any moment (larger flank tiles, less colour shift); three full-res frames as proof.
4. Remove the drawn outline and eye and let tile edges carry the silhouette, or correct the "no figure is drawn" claim.
5. Two deliberate holds and one burst of fast cuts timed to the choices (e.g. 3.42, 23.79), visible in a frame-difference plot; the colour change follows the score's trust.
Critic round 2 · PASS (narrow)
# SCION 04 · critic round 2 · PASS (narrow)

Fresh independent critic (all 768 scene frames at full resolution and zoomed into the dog, frame-to-frame difference measured, sound per window; judged before reading round 1).

Per choice: backing away reads (head low, slides away, a burst of change over the span); turned back reads, slightly weak (flips at 3.42–3.50 with a burst exactly at the choice; head hard to make out in the crouch); turning to look reads (rises 7.50, facing flips 8.17 with the score's turn; no cut accent at 7.46); coming head low weak (head only slightly below the back until ~22 s); lying across the sill reads (drops at 23.79 with a burst; the jamb splits the body, front in the light); looking in and staying reads (head turns end-on into the lit doorway, ears from behind, 26.5–28.2; settles and holds).

Bottom-up: mostly yes. No line is drawn; the dog's edge is tiny archive tiles; recognisable pictures on the flank (cats, lion, giraffe, the real dog); the words typeset in tiles; cut rhythm follows the choices (bursts at 1.0, 3.42, 23.79; holds at 9–14 and 29–32 s; boil on twos). Remaining gap: the shape is a computed mould the torrent fills, and the flank pictures sit in a rectangular strip. Made of the torrent in material and rhythm, not yet in form.

Round-1 conditions: 1 partly met (head low only from ~22 s); 2, 3, 4, 5 met.

Remaining (recorded, not pursued: stopped at an independent pass per the SCION doctrine): head below the withers from 14.7; a cut accent at 7.46; break the rectangular flank strip so pictures of mixed sizes run into the legs, head and edge; the jamb breaks the outline at 22–24 s; the person's face is a featureless disc.

method · every asset and its rights · ledger of attempts · build · the prompt

SCION 05Atmospheric multiplane · 38 s · PASS

Yuri Norstein

Depth, fog and focus decide what the dog can see, and what we can.

Made of

About twenty glass planes at measured depths: sky, trees, the room, the door, the house front, floors, posts and weeds; six fog layers keyed from public-domain time-lapse cloud footage; a bowl inside the room cut from the home movie. The dog is a library of hand-cut replacement pieces (five body views, crouch and lying stages, heads per view and ear state, leg segments, paws, tail), each traced once from a maquette, given a scissor edge and painted with the archive dog's coat grain.

Moved by

A camera that pans, drops and moves in, so parallax comes from real depth. The fog acts in four states: it thickens between door and dog, is drawn toward the door light, parts along the dog's path and hangs still. Focus is an event: it pulls into the room when the door opens and when the dog is told come in. The dog's pieces are chosen and hinged each frame and laid far to near on its own plane.

SCION 06Harmonic motion · 38 s · PASS

John Whitney

Trust is a fifth: the dog comes when its breath locks to the house.

Made of

Only computed points of light. A 3D skeleton whose proportions are the archive dog's measured rig keys; every part a tube of meridian dot-lines with its own harmonic number, winding into helices and unwinding into straight engraved lines every 8 s. The house, the room, the hearth and the person are point-lines too; the moon is a Lissajous trail of the two oscillators.

Moved by

The house pulses at 0.40 Hz and the dog breathes near 0.6 Hz. The calls detune the dog, so its lines go ragged and the moon smears; once the circumstances change the two oscillators couple and lock in a 3:2, and the dog's lines stand straight. The gait is a phase relation driven by distance travelled; the turns are true rotations; the sound is the same oscillator bank, a pure fifth when locked.

SCION 07Drawn metamorphosis · 38 s · PASS

Peter Foldès

The door's outline is lifted off the door and laid on the porch as the light.

Made of

Contour keys drawn as coordinates: 16 body keys and 10 head keys, each stroke with a fixed point count so that point i is the same place on the dog in every key. The archive dog was studied for proportion, not traced; the person, the house, the door and the dusk are drawn the same way.

Moved by

In-betweens are weighted blends of corresponding points, paced to the score; turns pass through drawn rear and front views where a symmetric key swaps its correspondences. Shapes change as consequences: the caller swells and the reaching arm is drawn long; the refusing dog closes into a curl; the door's outline is carried out onto the boards as its light.

SCION 08Constraint Sketchpad · 38 s · PASS (narrow)

Ivan Sutherland

THE DOG IS NOT SELECTABLE: the brief's 'understand it, not control it', said in Sketchpad's own terms.

Made of

Points, lines and arcs on a simulated phosphor vector display. The dog is a 24-point graph under 77 hard constraints (bone lengths, joint sides, floor contact, and the threshold itself), its proportions the archive dog's measured bones; nothing else is borrowed.

Moved by

A real iterative solver, warm-started every frame, with its residuals on the console. The prompts are light-pen drags: the reach pulls the hand toward the dog until the console says HAND GOAL UNSATISFIABLE. The dog cannot be dragged at all.

method · every asset and its rights · ledger of attempts · build · the prompt

SCION 09As-rigid-as-possible puppetry · 38 s · PASS (narrow)

Takeo Igarashi

A drawing that bends without ever folding: the dog keeps its shape by solving for it.

Made of

Six dog drawings drawn in code: a standing profile, standing and sitting back views, a lying sphinx profile, a profile head with collar and tag, and the back of the head. Each is cut into a triangle mesh, with cuts between the forelegs and at the tail notch, and the eye, nose, tag and buttons weighted forty times stiffer. The moon and trees are Quick, Draw! doodles.

Moved by

Only by an as-rigid-as-possible solver: handles, a best rotation per triangle, one energy minimized per frame; a triangle that flips is stiffened and the frame solved again. Feet come from a stepping simulation, planted or in the air, never slid. Turns swap whole drawings, bent across by the solver, the head leading. Against weighted skinning on the same mesh, the solver folds no triangles where skinning folds dozens.

SCION 10One sheet warped by handles · 38 s · PASS

MLS deformation

There is no skeleton anywhere: the dog bends as one sheet, the way a hide would if handles pulled it.

Made of

A single flat image of a dog's coat, painted in code and laid out like a pelt: dark saddle, both flanks, four legs, head and tail. No archive frame, no skeleton, no cut pieces, no frame swaps. The swarm wrote the moving-least-squares solver itself, in its affine, similarity and rigid forms, and the end card shows the hide with its handles and the same pose in all three.

Moved by

Only 48 point handles and 19 line handles move. Affine MLS lifts the flat hide into a body each frame: the flanks fold down like cloth, the legs reach the boards, the head is a rigid cluster of handles so the face does not shear. Paws stay planted until the body moves too far, then step on alternating diagonals.

SCION 11Footage cut-outs moved as puppets · 38 s · PASS (narrow)

Video puppetry

Every pixel of the dog is the real dog; only the way its pieces are held changes.

Made of

Two frames of the public-domain home-movie dog (Amateur Film New Jersey C), cut out with their masks into a body sheet, a rigid head and neck, a tail and four legs; a three-quarter frame of the same dog is swapped in for the turns, a head turned toward the door is cut from the footage for the look back, and a back-of-head piece is cut from the dog's own dark fur with a drawn mask. SCION 12Bounded biharmonic weights on a lace mesh · 38 s · PASS (narrow)

Localized weights

Legs a few millimetres apart on a sheet full of holes move independently because the weights stay local across every gap.

Made of

One cut-paper profile of a dog drawn in code, pierced with lace holes, legs 10–25 mm apart and the ears side by side, in a cut-away dollhouse view of the doorway at dusk; the person is made the same way. No kit or archive material; all sound is synthesized.

Moved by

A triangle mesh (5,565 vertices) with 12 bones, 2 point handles and a 6-vertex torso cage; bounded biharmonic weights solved here (cotangent Laplacian, active-set solve), bounded, summing to one and mostly exactly zero, then linear blend skinning. Lifting one foreleg moves the other 0 mm, against 66 mm with naive weights; the end card shows the heat maps.

- **Looking in and staying:** weak to reads. At 26.9–28.1 the back of the head faces the viewer, so it reads in depth. But one orange ear streams flat into the room like a flag, ambiguous at full frame. Settles by 28.2 and stays.

**Legibility:** the head keeps a legible dog in every frame, with no tangles. Weak points:
- frontal "puffball" frames at 3.63 and 8.38;
- while walking and lying the body reads as a hanging fringe, like a mop or caterpillar with a dog's head;
- the legs rarely show.

**Language:** built bottom-up: primary trajectories, spring-damper strips under gravity, wind and the door draft. The coat sleeks and settles, the curtain blows and the reeds sway. The dog is native (torn tissue strips on discs) and distinct from PAE-00 and 17. It is close to 41 in mechanism but not in material.

**Craft:**
- The title overflows the right edge in a fallback serif, and an end-card line overflows.
- "Forepaws in" is not visible.
- Curtain strips draw over the dog at 26–28.
- The white muzzle strips read as fangs at 24.4–25.
- The coat is busy on the walk.

**Sound:** voices start before each reaction. Calls −23.5/−23.2 dB, soft voice −34.5/−33.6/−35.8 dB, room tone about −43 dB. **Rights:** clean (all synthesized, OFL fonts).

**Verdict:** PASS, narrowly. The narrow points touch the brief ("a dog in every frame" and looking in depth), so under the doctrine the swarm gets one more repair round.

**Repair conditions (ranked)**
1. During the look-in (26.6–28.2) no ear strip projects horizontally into the room past the muzzle line; the back of the head or the muzzle alone carries the look.
2. While lying (24.2–32) at least one folded foreleg or paw is visible, or "forepaws in" is removed from the end card.
3. During the approach, legs or feet are visible below the fringe in at least half the frames from 18 to 20 s.
4. The title and end-card text fit inside 1280 px and use the credited fonts.
5. No curtain strip draws over the dog's body at 26–28 s.

SCION 14Nora Willett lineage · 38 s · PASS

Pose2Pose casting

The performance is a path through a drawer of drawings: casting, not animating.

Made of

An illustration bank drawn for this film: 41 ink-and-wash dog drawings set by hand from a construction kit (profile, three-quarter, front and back heads, legs, tail, a dark saddle, a pale ruff and socks), and 13 drawings of the person; the porch, door, room, light and sky drawn as paths. The archive dog was studied for build and markings; no pixel of it is used.

Moved by

Only by choosing one whole drawing per frame: each score frame becomes a pose vector, and a Viterbi path picks drawings by pose distance plus a transition cost, so turns pass through the drawn three-quarter and front views and nothing flickers. Walk and backing drawings sit on a gait chart so planted paws stay pinned. Nothing is tweened or warped.

Learned layered puppet

The end card shows what the model makes alone next to what had to be corrected by hand.

Discipline is what is not rigged: the drawing does the acting, the rig only carries it.

Made of

Every drawing written as control points for this film: tapered brush-ribbon lines with fixed pressure, flat colour with a synthesized watercolour wash and pooled edge. A cut-away house with a patterned room, a hearth and a carved door frame; a dusk porch with spiral-canopy trees. The dog is a turnaround and expression sheet: profile standing, crouching and lying, three-quarter rear, rear, and five heads. Original designs, not the studio's.

Moved by

Views swap by substitution, on twos; a restrained rig moves only the legs (two-bone, paws planted), the tail and the neck. One push-in to the threshold, with line weight held constant through the zoom. The room stays dim until the door opens, and the light falls from the doorway as a wedge onto the boards.

SCION 19Smart-Bone rig with switch layers · 38 s · PASS

Moho Smart Bones

The turns are not tweened: a view bone switches whole drawings while corrective shapes re-draw the joints it bends.

Made of

One variable-width ink line over flat fills on paper grain, drawn in code by the swarm: an ochre dog with a cream bib, muzzle and socks, a house shown in section, a person drawn the same way. Five head drawings (profile, three-quarter front, front, three-quarter back, back) from one landmark sheet. No kit or archive material is used; all sound is synthesized.

Moved by

A Moho-style rig of 25 bones (spine, neck, head, two view-control bones, three-bone IK legs, tail and ears). Six Smart-Bone corrective actions are driven by bone angles: the neck turn bulges the shoulder and shortens the neck, the spine curls, the legs fold round the haunch and elbow, and the chin flattens on the paws. Switch layers pick the head view, and a footfall planner keeps weight-bearing feet planted.

SCION 20Production rig: drawing substitution and node graph · 38 s · PASS

Toon Boom Harmony

Every turn is a substitution that lands on a whole drawing, never a dissolve; the end card shows the real xsheet rows of the head-lead turn.

Made of

A library of 106 drawings made in code: 57 dog bodies (stand, crouch, three walk cycles, lowering, lying and front, back and three-quarter views), 9 heads including a back-of-head drawing for looking in, ears, tails, person poses and mouths. Ink, tone and rim-highlight passes over a painted dusk porch and a cutaway lit room, with a palette of 27 named colour styles. No kit or archive material; all sound is synthesized.

Moved by

A 26-node graph (exposure sheet, library, palette, pegs, element nodes, a neck curve deformer, a tail chain, composites, cast shadow, grade) evaluated each frame. The acting is an exposure sheet shipped as xsheet.json and .tsv: holds on twos, walks on ones, walk drawings chosen by distance travelled so planted feet do not slide.

SCION 21Photographic actor rigged from stills · 38 s · PASS (narrow)

Photo-to-Actor

A dog that only ever walked to a bowl is retargeted, from a handful of stills, to perform all six choices of the score.

Made of

Six frames of the public-domain home movie Amateur Film New Jersey C with their SAM masks: torso, neck, leg segments and tail cut from them and laid on a body template, and five angle heads (front, three-quarter, profile, lowered, back of head). The person, door leaf and lit hall come from Straight Talk (NTIS, public domain), the sky from Top of the Cloud; the house and light are computed and graded to the same grain.

Moved by

Landmarks fitted to each head (the automatic fit was accepted on the front and three-quarter heads and corrected by hand on the profile, lowered and back views, with the failures recorded), expression patches for ears, brow and mouth, whole-body sprite replacement for the turns with hard cuts, and two-bone IK keeping planted paws still.

SCION 22Performance puppet with behaviours · 38 s · PASS

Character Animator

The puppet is never animated frame by frame: it is performed, and the performance is a file of takes.

Made of

A digital felt the swarm made (a synthesized fibre texture, fuzzy cut edges, drop shadows between layers, running stitches), cut into named groups exported like a PSD puppet, with swap sets for the views: side, three-quarter and back bodies; profile, front, back three-quarter and back heads. The person and the place are made the same way; no kit or archive material is used.

Moved by

A small runtime with Character Animator's behaviours: triggers, view swaps that cut rather than tween, gaze snapping to named targets, blink and breathe, dangle physics on the ears, tail and ponytail, planted-foot walking, and lip sync of the person from the voice track into the twelve standard mouth shapes. The dog's acting is five layered takes of timed control tracks; the end card says no camera or live performer exists here.

SCION 23Skeletal runtime with mixed action clips · 38 s · PASS (narrow)

Spine runtime

The head leads because it plays on its own track over the body; the end card draws the real track timeline.

Made of

Painted game-art parts made in code (tan coat, dark saddle, banded gouache shading, brush strokes, a broken outline) packed into one texture atlas of 46 regions: seven head views, four body views as weighted meshes, ears, and lit and shaded leg segments. The set and the person are painted the same way; all sound is synthesized.

Moved by

A Spine-shaped skeleton JSON (28 bones, 20 slots in draw order, four two-bone IK legs) and a library of ten action clips (idle, cringe-back, turn-away, lie, look-back, rise, stalk-walk, lie-across, look-in, settle), mixed by a runtime written here on a body track with a head track layered over it. Turns are attachment swaps and slot reorders through 0, 45, 90 and 135 degree views.

SCION 24Portrait volume illusion · 38 s · PASS

Live2D lineage

Gaze, head and body are three separate decisions, and the dog makes them in that order.

Made of

A parametric rig written from scratch (no Cubism software here): 17 parameter axes in the Live2D style; a head keyform table of 16 yaw by 5 pitch keys over named landmarks, blended bilinearly; body art meshes with saddle, blaze and belly markings on a chain of rotation deformers; eye, brow and mouth controls; the coat studied by eye from the archive dog. The person is a small rig in the same language; the house is drawn in cross-section.

Moved by

The eyes move first, the head two or three frames later, the body last. Both turns pass through a chest-facing view rather than a mirror flip, the head leading; a stepping solver keeps paws fixed until they must step; pendulums drive the tail, ears and tag. The camera zooms about fixed points, with a portrait of the dog while it considers.

SCION 25Interactive puppet state machines · 38 s · PASS

Rive

The score is played into the puppet as inputs; the dog's choices come out of its own state machine.

Made of

An artboard of capsule shapes bound to bones: a two-capsule torso with tuck-up, neck, skull, muzzle, curved-triangle ears, a five-segment tapered tail and four two-bone IK legs, with the coat pattern clipped to the torso. Keyed poses (stand, crouch, lie, rest, look in) and looping clips (breath, wag) are mixed by 1D blend states. The house is drawn in section under an oblique camera. Proportions were studied from a public-domain home movie; no archive pixels are used. Rive itself was not available, so the swarm wrote a small runtime with Rive's primitives.

Moved by

Typed inputs (numbers, triggers, a boolean) feed three layered state machines: Facing, Body and Attention. Only Facing may rotate the body, and both turns go through the dog's front. A stepping solver plants each foot until its home moves too far, and the head layer leads the body round at 7.46 s.

SCION 26GPU scene graph of archive fragments · 38 s · PASS

PixiJS archive stage

Hundreds of small fragments, texture-batched, are held together into one animal by masks and render-texture passes.

Made of

966 fragments in one 2048 px texture atlas, cut from public-domain archive shots in the kit (coat from the archive dog's own masks, pale fur from a lion, dark ears from a tiger), Quick, Draw! drawings and own work; atlas.json records each fragment's source, frame, crop and mask.

Moved by

PixiJS 7.4.2 in headless Chromium: six depth layers, an alpha mask cut to the door, five render textures per frame (dog body, dog head, person, light, stage), a threshold-and-outline shader that turns fragment clouds into a silhouette, and a grain pass; 31 draw calls a frame. The fragments are posed each frame on a skeleton with planted feet.

SCION 27Declarative film compiler · 38 s · PASS

Remotion

The film is a pure function of its description: render it twice and every frame is the same.

Made of

Real Remotion 4 with React, installed in the folder: the score compiled into a typed manifest, one record per frame, that fails on any missing value; frame-pure React components that draw each frame as SVG from its record alone; a dog of 34 tapered capsules and two ear triangles on a 3-D joint graph, its proportions the medians of the archive dog's rig keys; a matte composition of the same dog for measurement. All sound synthesized as events.

Moved by

Position along the porch is exactly the score's x; a stepping solver keeps each paw fixed until a step carries it; capsules seen end-on are discs, so both turns pass through the face. Ten frames rendered twice give identical hashes. The licence tier for Remotion's free use is flagged for the operator to confirm.

Critic round 1 · PASS
# SCION 27 · critic round 1 · PASS

Independent critic (fresh agent; frames every 0.5 s and every frame in the turn and look-in windows, full-resolution crops; the build inspected only afterwards, to judge the language).

All six choices read on time: backing away (crouch facing the door, drifts away; a hunched lump with the head sunk at 1.4–2.0); turned back (a true frontal view 3.62, three-quarter 3.71, faces away by 3.79, lies facing away from 4.5 as the score has it, to 7.4); turning to look (the head swings round at 7.54; one blob frame at 7.71; frontal/three-quarter facing the door 7.79–7.88); coming head low (below the back line ~14.6–23.5); lying across the sill (a true sphinx lie, forelegs and head inside the jamb, hip and tail out on the porch); looking in and staying, in depth (the back of the skull to the viewer, the muzzle into the lit room, 26.5–27.4). A dog in every frame; no edge-on collapse.

Language: built bottom-up in the pipeline: a typed manifest compiled from the score, frame-pure React/SVG components, a camera module, seeded randomness, synthesized audio events; no Math.random, Date.now or effects in the source. The dog is a 3-D capsule joint graph, but the capsules are the drawn primitive itself, typed components evaluated per frame, not a guide with a style poured over it: native for a swarm whose language is the pipeline. Its weakness: the look is generic flat vector; the proof lives in determinism and data, not in the image. Fundamentally different from PAE-00.

Defects: the approach 14.7–23.8 is effectively silent (~−51 to −58 dB RMS, no audible paws, though the builder reports paw plants as sound events); bulky bear-like hindquarters when crouched and passing the person; blob frames at 1.4 and 7.71; the "Come in." caption nearly illegible; a faceless person. Soft voice ~11–12 dB under the calls, ~25 dB above room tone.

Rights: everything made here; Remotion's bundled licence confirms a free tier for individuals, non-profits and companies of up to 3 employees; whether Bolas qualifies needs an operator decision. Reproducibility claimed on one machine only; the lockfile lives only in the git-ignored cache.

Remaining (not pursued: stopped at an independent pass per the SCION doctrine): audible paws through the approach; head and muzzle separate from the body at 1.2–2.3 and 7.6–7.8; a readable "Come in." caption; a committed lockfile and a second-machine hash check; an operator ruling on the Remotion licence.

Operator ruling (Remotion licence): the operator is an individual, so the free tier applies. Recorded here; no change to the film.

SCION 28Compositor's film of archive plates · 38 s · PASS (narrow)

Mask and plate foundry

Plates from different films are pinned, held out and lit until they share one shot, and the dog performs by animating its masks.

Made of

Public-domain archive plates from the kit: Amateur Film New Jersey C (the dog, the facade and porch, stabilised and median-stacked into a clean plate), Straight Talk (door, room and standing person), Survival Stresses (the seated person) and Door to Heaven (1941, the arm). One standing dog frame is split by masks into body, legs and head; six of the dog's own turn frames carry the turns.

Moved by

A layer compositor written for the film: keyframed bezier masks with feather and expand, parented transforms, corner pins from one locked camera, an inverted camera track, holdout mattes at the door frame and sill, luma keys, roto, and one door-light function lighting floor, sill, dog and person. The score drives the dog's root track; feet lock to the floor by distance travelled.

# SCION 28 · critic round 1 · DOES NOT PASS

A fresh, independent critic judged the film from the picture and sound only. It did not open method.md, the ledger, proof/ or build/.

**Per choice (scene times)**
- **Backing away:** reads. 1.0–2.4: the dog faces the door and travels right with a stride.
- **Turned back:** weak. Real archive turn plates at 3.46–3.92 are soft: 3.46 has no legible head and 3.875 shows a ghosted double head. The dog is in profile away by 3.96 and lies from 4.5, but it reads as a lumpy heap rather than a back.
- **Turning to look:** weak. The head moves at 7.50 and the muzzle points at the door while the body is down, but the head sits on the hips, like a second animal on the dog's back.
- **Coming, head low:** reads. The head snaps low (14.667→14.708) and stays below the back through 23.6, with no clear sliding at 18–20.
- **Lying across the sill:** weak. The dog lies parallel to the wall across the doorway, so inside and outside cannot be told apart. No tail is visible, there is a seam mid-body at 24.5, and the legs only partly read as folded.
- **Looking in and staying:** weak. A dark back-of-head plate at 26.5–28.2 points into the room but is separated from the body by a gap with room light showing through (27.0).

**Not a legible dog in every frame:**
- 3.46: no head.
- 3.875: double head.
- 7.5–7.9: the head displaced onto the hips.
- 8.0–8.42: two heads at once.

**Language:** a genuine composite built bottom-up: a tracked median clean plate, corner-pinned door and room, roto and luma-keyed people, holdout mattes and a light pass. The dog is native to the idea, and it is distinct from 11, 29, 31, 45 and 53. But the bar (no edge halos, stable matte boundaries, grain and light agreeing) is not met on the dog.

**Craft:**
- The dog is cool, over-bright and haloed with no contact shadow in the dusk section.
- A dashed white matte line runs along the sill.
- The wall is a tiled block mosaic, and the room floor reads like stairs.
- The person is a different body in each stage.
- The arm is static and kinks at the shoulder.
- Audio passes: calls at about −23 dB, the soft voice at −35 to −38 dB, room tone −53 dB.

**Rights:** OK. Amateur Film New Jersey C, Straight Talk (NTIS), Survival Stresses and Door to Heaven (1941) are all public domain per the kit. Identifiable people come only from PD films.

**Repair conditions (ranked)**
1. At 8.0–8.45 only one head is visible.
2. From 7.46 to 8.0 the look-back head attaches at the neck end of the lying body, not on the hips.
3. At 3.46–3.92 each frame shows exactly one legible head, with no ghosting.
4. From 24 s the dog lies across the sill, not along it: a clear part of the body past the jamb inside, the tail visibly on the porch outside, and no mid-body seam. The look-in head joins the neck with no gap.
5. Before the door light, the dog's luma, edge softness and contact shadow match the porch (no halo), and the dashed matte line on the sill is gone.

SCION 29Cutout extraction · 38 s · PASS (narrow)

GrabCut

The cut is the film: every edge of the dog is an argument about where it ends.

Made of

A graph cut written for this film (Boykov–Kolmogorov max-flow), seeded but not decided by the kit's SAM masks, with scribbles from the rig keys, a 2× supersampled cut, colour and texture models and a clean plate of the shot with the dog removed. Nine frames of the home-movie dog, cut and saved with contour and failure masks; the woman and door cut from Straight Talk, the hand from The Door to Heaven, the wall and boards from the dog's own shot.

Moved by

Re-performance of the cuts: the dog's real walk mirrored and run backward for the retreat and chosen by distance for the approach; its own turning frames for the refusal; a lying dog assembled from one frame's cut pieces, with the hidden belly a recorded repair; the back of the head from the one frame the home movie shot from behind.

SCION 30Evidence-bounded mask proposals · 38 s · PASS (narrow)

SAM 2 proposals

Nothing is drawn: when the archive has no evidence of a pose, the film stages around it instead of inventing it.

Made of

Precomputed SAM 2.1 mask proposals from public-domain archive films in the kit: 48 accepted frames of the dog in Amateur Film New Jersey C under one object ID, the door from Straight Talk, a hand from Door to Heaven (1941) and a sitter from Survival Stresses. Recorded correction strokes and evidence refusals: no frame shows the dog lying, so its legs are hidden behind a porch board rather than made up.

Moved by

Substitution of real frames placed by their measured centroids on the score: the source dog's own step and turn played forward and backward, a confidence proxy (stability times edge agreement) that rejected frames which leak, and a contact sheet of every source frame with its object ID and confidence on the end card.

SCION 31Soft-edged matted archive frames · 38 s · DOES NOT PASS (narrowly) · in repair after critic round 4

Alpha Matting

The dog's edges hold against sky and siding, because the matte, not the outline, decides what is dog.

Made of

Every archive frame of the dog is matted with a fur-class alpha, so the edges are fractional, not cut. The woman and the door come from another public-domain film; the lying dog is cut from one matte and labelled as reconstruction.

Moved by

By swapping whole matted frames, and by pivoting pieces of a single matte. Premultiplied compositing keeps the edges from picking up the background.

SCION 32Jointed toy moved only by forward kinematics · 38 s · PASS

Forward kinematics

The joint graph you see is the one that moves it: change a hip and exactly its children follow, saddle cloth and all.

Made of

Cream-painted carved blocks on brass pins, a red felt saddle cloth with two flaps, black drop ears and a red and cream bead tail, rendered in WebGL; a peg-figure person with a cardigan. Everything is made in code; all sound is synthesized.

Moved by

A 31-joint graph for the dog and 25 for the person, every world transform the parent times a pivot offset times local rotations, root first, with no IK anywhere. The head pivot has view anchors for profile, three-quarter and rear, blended by angle. Feet are planted only by hand-keyed hip and knee angles, and the remaining foot slip is measured and stated on the end card.

SCION 33Joints, chains and planted effectors · 38 s · PASS

Inverse kinematics

A command is a target the arm cannot reach; the threshold is a target the dog's own chain lays down across.

Made of

A shepherd-type rig: pelvis and chest, neck, skull and muzzle, ears, a seven-link tail and four three-segment legs, drawn as what it is (tapered capsules with rings at the elbows, stifles and hocks) and lit by dusk and the door light. Proportions come only as numbers from the archive dog's rig keys; no archive pixel appears. The person is built in the same language, and the door is a rigid body with friction.

Moved by

Analytic two-bone IK with pole vectors and joint limits on every leg, every frame, checked against a FABRIK solver. A footfall planner locks each planted paw in world space and lifts it only when its home has moved or the leg can no longer hold it. The head aims at world targets: the person, the lit boards, the low hand, the room. The person's reach for the dog straightens toward the target and falls metres short, while the reach to the door edge arrives and pushes it open.

The sheets

SCION 34Engraved mesh, shape kept by a local–global solve · 38 s · PASS

As-rigid-as-possible

The dog bends where its triangles can keep their shape and nowhere else.

Made of

The dog, the woman and the place are hatch engravings drawn in code, the hatching running along the limbs. The dog is a Delaunay mesh (profile, rear, front, lying and head-turned-in rest shapes), with proportions taken as numbers from the public-domain archive dog. The swarm wrote the Sorkine–Alexa local–global solve itself, with sparse factorisation; the end card shows the rest meshes and their handles.

Moved by

About twenty handles at shoulder, hip, neck, tail, paws and ankles drive the solve, which decides every leg bend, the neck, the belly and the ear folds. View changes are single-frame swaps between rest meshes, hidden as the outgoing drawing narrows. On the same handles, the solve keeps every lying frame free of inverted triangles where a naive warp folds dozens.

SCION 35Risograph print bent by moving least squares · 38 s · PASS

MLS gesture warp

The warp is honest about itself: wherever the print is bent, its dot screen visibly stretches.

Made of

A two-colour risograph print made in code: an orange coat plate and a blue shade and key-line plate, screened in the dog's own print space, in 13 view prints (profile, three-quarter, rear, lying, look-back, look-in). The porch is screened in screen space at a finer pitch. No kit material; all sound is synthesized.

Moved by

37 named control anchors with typed moving-least-squares families per layer (rigid skull and ears, similarity legs, tail and neck, affine coat), inverse-distance falloff, a fold guard, and print swaps at the midpoint of fast swings with no cross-dissolve. The dot-grid strain is measured every frame.

No one paints a weight: the found silhouette rigs itself.

SCION 37One skin over a skeleton, weighted per vertex · 38 s · PASS

Weighted skinning

The joints keep their girth because of how the skin is blended, and the film shows the version that does not.

Made of

A single closed triangle mesh (about 7,200 vertices) grown by surface nets over a signed-distance sculpture on a 27-bone rest skeleton, with weights from heat diffusion on the cotangent Laplacian (the Pinocchio method), pruned to four bones per vertex. The coat is some 16,000 small patches of film pixels from the public-domain home-movie dog, kept hard-edged so the archive's grain shows. The person is a second skinned mesh in a wool coat.

Moved by

Linear blend skinning and dual-quaternion skinning are both implemented; the film uses dual quaternions. A two-panel inset at the lie-down shows linear blend losing volume at the hock beside the fix, and the end card adds the weight maps and a 160° neck-twist stress test. Feet step on a gait clock with two-bone IK and do not slide.

The sheets

Did the description survive?

Measured from this swarm's own render (its trace) by check_handmade.py. The score's time, then the time the choice appears.

backs away 1.00 → 0.96 swatches 2.33 → 2.33 sturns its back 3.42 → 3.38 swatches 7.46 → 7.54 scomes, head low 14.71 → 14.79 slies across the sill 23.79 → 23.96 s

SCION 38Drawing library · 38 s · PASS (narrow)

Dynamic pose selection

The dog that turns its back is the same drawing as the dog that comes.

Made of

Real people's Quick, Draw! dog doodles, chosen by eye from 4,164 and labelled by pose; the house, door, moon, steps, arm and hand are doodles too, drawn on in their makers' stroke order.

Moved by

A decoder picks a whole drawing for every pair of frames, with costs for pose, for changing identity and for swapping off a footfall. A drawing is only scaled, moved or mirrored, never bent.

SCION 39Turn lab · 38 s · PASS

Multiview substitution

It solves the turns, the place every earlier swarm failed, by never pretending the dog is a solid.

Made of

Separate drawings made in code for this film: three body views (profile, rear three-quarter, rear), lying versions of the rear two, and four head views, the other side by flipping a near-symmetric dog (dark saddle, pale collar ring, dark tail). The archive dog gave markings and proportions only.

Moved by

A turn is a swap to the next view, never a squash or a rotation: the head goes first, the body follows one view at a time, the feet step at each swap, and the torso stays on the score's x through every swap. The door is the only thing that rotates, because a door is flat.

method · every asset and its rights · ledger of attempts · build · the prompt

SCION 40Paper performance, captured and replayed · 38 s · PASS

Tabletop capture

The acting exists only as data recovered from fingers moving paper, and the film replays it.

Made of

No camera, table or performer existed, and the film says so: a synthesized overhead tabletop plate of white-card puppets with printed fiducial tabs, moved by public-domain archive hands from The Door to Heaven (1941) matted by their SAM masks. The film itself uses new paper skins cut here: 37 body cards and 11 head cards on a cut-away house at dusk. All sound is synthesized.

Moved by

Every piece is tracked from the plate's pixels alone: corner-fiducial calibration, per-card fiducials refined by Lucas–Kanade on the card's own printed texture with hand pixels weighted out, texture identification when a hand hides a marker, and hand mattes from a clean-plate difference plus a learned skin model. The recovered rigid transforms and a 358-event stream drive the film; a corner inset shows the capture with its tracks and mattes.

SCION 41Inertia and restraint · 38 s · PASS

Secondary dynamics

Nothing secondary is animated by hand, so stillness has to be earned: the holds are quiet because the energy has gone.

Made of

A dog solved and drawn in code from spheres and capsules: six torso spheres, neck and head, muzzle, four legs, two ear flaps, a seven-node tail, a collar with a tag; proportions and coat studied by eye from the archive dog, no archive pixel in the film. The person has a scarf; the door is wood on a hinge; there is a shrub.

Moved by

The primary motion follows the score, with planted feet that step. Everything secondary is solved, never keyframed: ears, tail, coat, tag, scarf, shrub and the door are springs and dampers pulled toward the pose, colliding with bodies, floor and lap, and dying away in the holds. The person's one push is sized so the door reaches its stop at 6.20 s.

Bottom-up: spheres and capsules on springs with real follow-through (tail tuck, ears flop on the head turn, the tag swings, the scarf settles); energy dies away in the holds. Self-made; only proportions measured from the public-domain kit.

Round-2 conditions: 1 met (the leaf visible in every frame, no pop); 2 met (legs near vertical in the holds); 3 partly met (optional).

Remaining (optional, not pursued: stopped at an independent pass per the SCION doctrine): the held look-in gaze at about the person's head height; near and far legs coincide in side views (two legs show); the neck thins at 26.8–27.0; the open door is a thin edge-on sliver.

SCION 44Spatial collage in depth, with a moving camera · 38 s · PASS

Multiplane parallax

The room behind the door is a real deeper plane, so looking in has somewhere to go.

Made of

Fourteen collage planes at measured depths, from dusk clouds 140 m away (Top Of The Cloud) through trees, a real room behind the door (back wall, lamp, a hinged door leaf from Straight Talk), the clapboard wall at 0 m, the porch and a near railing. The dog is thirty cut-paper pieces textured from the home-movie dog, each set at its own depth so it has parallax of its own; the moon is a Quick, Draw! drawing, CC BY 4.0, credited.

Moved by

The camera never rotates: it cranes down, trucks with the approach, and pushes through the railing to the doorway as the dog looks in, so depth is carried by how fast each plane moves. A foot planner keeps planted feet still; the pieces re-stack as the dog turns.

SCION 45Who is in front? · 38 s · PASS (narrow)

Occlusion compositing

Half in, half out is a fact about layers: the door frame is in front of one half of the dog and behind the other.

Made of

Every element an RGBA layer with a depth, ordered each frame by which layers overlap and which is nearer, and drawn back to front. The dog is the real dog of Amateur Film New Jersey C: its own frames cut by their mattes, its own turn played for the refusal, a lying pose built from cut parts. The hallway and door from Straight Talk, the hand from The Door to Heaven, the bulb and sky found; the house, porch, post and the person drawn.

Moved by

The thesis is the ending: the dog lies at an angle into the doorway, its depth crossing the plane of the door frame under the post, so it is split into two layers, the front half behind the post in the doorway and the hindquarters in front on the porch. The door holds back the room's light until it opens; the porch post passes in front of the walking dog; the layer order never loops or flips in any frame.

# SCION 45 · critic round 1 · DOES NOT PASS

Independent critic (fresh agent; ~300 frames including every frame in the turn, lie and look-in windows; audio measured; verdict from the picture before any builder notes).

Per choice: backing away reads (faces the door, slides away, head low, after the call; a legless lump at 2.03 and a double-exposed tangle at 2.28); turned back weak (a box with legs at 3.51, two heads at 3.55; frontal 3.63–4.09 reads; a clear profile facing away only at 4.13; the lying refusal a jagged splayed assembly 5.0–7.4); turning to look weak (the head rises straight up like a seal or giraffe neck 7.63–7.88, faces the door only ~8.3; a two-cel tangle at 8.18); coming head low reads (but one headless "boulder" cel recurs in the walk at 16.5, 18.0, 18.75, 19.5, 20.25, 22.5); lying across the sill weak (legs fold on time with a good cast shadow; flipper-like forelegs; see the jamb defect); looking in does not read (an edge-on sliver at 26.49, then a featureless pale disc with a matte halo 26.53–27.99, no ear, muzzle or skull); staying reads. Not a dog in every frame.

Language: genuinely the swarm's: real archive cels cut by their mattes, split into sublayers and re-ordered; the porch post passes in front of the dog with the correct order and no pops; the hall light held out until the door opens. Not PAE-00, not replayed footage. But the defining occlusion at the sill is incoherent.

Defects: the jamb defect (biggest): at 28.0 the dog's paws and belly rest on the porch about 50 px below the sill line, yet the door-casing post occludes the top of its back from 23.75 to the end; a body in front of the sill cannot be behind a casing standing on it. Wrong layer order at the defining moment; the end card's "behind the jamb and in front of it" does not match the picture. Also: a squared vertical matte edge on the hindquarters; light halos on the head layer; no contact shadows on the dark porch; the person cropped off-frame at 7.8. Audio good: soft voice 10–12 dB under the calls, well above room tone. Rights clean.

Repair conditions (ranked):
1. 23.8–31.5: every dog pixel occluded by the jamb casing sits at or behind the sill line; otherwise the dog is in front of the casing with a clear portion of its body inside the frame on the sill or interior floor.
2. 26.4–28.4: no head frame narrower than ~40% of its rest width; the turned head shows an ear or muzzle into the lit hall.
3. 3.42–4.6 and 7.46–8.6: a single dog with one head and four-leg support in every frame; the head toward the door by 7.9 without the vertical neck; a clear facing-away profile by 3.9.

SCION 47Viseme and gesture · 38 s · PASS

Sound-to-action

The dog answers sounds, not lines: each choice is a measured lag after a vowel.

Made of

The seven spoken lines synthesized with CMU Flite, whose own phoneme timing is the film's clock; ten profile mouth shapes for the person; a dog of 172 distinct inked drawings in side, three-quarter and rear views; arcs at the mouth and at the dog's ear and paw ticks computed from the audio; every other sound synthesized and panned by screen position.

Moved by

A typed list of sound events places each line so that the vowel the dog answers, plus a hearing latency, lands on the score's choice; an exposure sheet names every frame's drawing, mouth, door and light. Drawings change on twos and the body moves only when a new drawing is shown, so planted feet stay planted; the turns pass through drawn views head first.

SCION 48A dog whose refusal is its own decision · 38 s · PASS

State machines

Nobody keyed the refusal: the dog decides it, and the board clacks when it does.

Made of

The whole scene is a display board of 320×180 flip-discs and LEDs: what gives light is lit (the sky, the room), what receives light is flipped (dog, person, house). The dog is 27 rounded segments, posed each frame by the machine's body layer and cast into the discs with dark outlines, eye discs and a head line. The spoken words type out on a strip of the board; state names never appear.

Moved by

A hierarchical utility state machine ticking 24 times a second, with drives (pressure, fear, memory of being pressed, curiosity, trust) and senses (it hears all round, but sees only where its head points, so with its back turned it misses the door and the low hand). Eleven parameters were tuned until its own choices fell on the score's times; the score's states are never an input. A relay clacks at each decision; the end card shows the states it took and the drive curves.

The sheets

SCION 49Museum open-access objects, cut and recomposed · 38 s · PASS

Open-content atelier

No line is drawn: every figure is a museum object, recomposed as an actor, with its object number on the card.

Made of

Every actor is a Smithsonian American Art Museum object marked CC0 on both the record and the image: the dog is the pointer from Currier & Ives, Woodcock Shooting (1973.122.4), cut into head, neck, torso, tail and two-part legs; the person is an Edouart cut-paper silhouette (1981.172.33) with an arm from another (1981.172.31); the door is José Dolores López's Screen Door (1989.30.3); the room is from E. F. Andrews's Interior of a House (1916.6.39); the dusk is MacGilvary's Twilight after the Rain (1913.3.3). The Getty hosts were refused by the proxy, so no Getty object is used, and the end card says so.

Moved by

The same pieces of the one print are hinged differently for every pose: the creeping stalk of the original print for the approach, the head mirrored about the neck to look back over the shoulder, a fifteen-frame lowering into the lie, and the engraved head reaching level past the jamb into the lit room.

SCION 50Library of Congress lineage · 38 s · PASS

Historical print stage

Every frame says whether the dog in it was photographed or invented.

Made of

One plate, Pl. I, The Threshold: a sectional elevation of a wall, doorway and porch at dusk, line-engraved and hand-coloured. The dog is traced by hand from one frame of Amateur Film New Jersey C into eleven pieces, each engraved with a line screen from the scan's own tone; both turns are the film's own frames, some printed as reversed impressions. The door from Straight Talk, the hand from The Door to Heaven. No Library of Congress item is used or claimed.

Moved by

Pieces rotate about hand-placed joints, legs by two-bone IK with stance feet pinned. Under the image a key shows the scan frame in use, its source and rights, and the state of the drawing: as photographed, printed reversed, or, in red with red pins at the joints, re-posed as invented motion.

SCION 51Montage on a measured cutting score · 38 s · PASS

Mouris montage research

The rhythm of the cuts carries the drama while the dog stays one animal.

Made of

44 crops from 39 public-domain kit frames, tagged by imagery class (fur, hard, eyes, paws, doors, lamps, hands, voices, soft) and temperature, cut flat with a paper margin and a scissor line into the dog's body, neck, head, legs and tail and into the person; cut-letter words. The Mouris collection itself was not viewed or used, and the cards say so; an invisible sphere pose guides the outline, as the end card states.

Moved by

A cutting score of whole-image substitutions over the scene: two-frame holds of hard, cold images on the calls, long warm holds on the soft voice, one cut per footfall on the approach; the narrated words appear as letters cut from class-matched images at their spoken frames.

# SCION 51 · critic round 1 · DOES NOT PASS

A fresh, independent critic judged the film from the picture and sound only. It did not open method.md, the ledger, proof/, build/, inventory.json or cutting-score.json.

**Per choice (scene times)**
- **Backing away:** reads. The call starts at 1.01, and the dog crouches facing the door and moves back by 1.08–1.20.
- **Turned back:** reads. A 3D turn at 3.54–3.79 with a true rear view at 3.67–3.71, then profile away. Lying facing away 5.0–7.4.
- **Turning to look:** reads. The head lifts at 7.42 and comes round toward the door by 7.58 while the body is down.
- **Coming, head low:** reads. Below the back by 14.88 through 23.7, with alternating legs and no obvious slide.
- **Lying across the sill:** reads, weakly. Forepaws and head past the sill from 24.25, tail outside. The hind legs stretch backwards rather than fold.
- **Looking in and staying:** reads in depth. The back of the head and the ears face into the lit room from 26.75 to 31.9.

A legible dog is in every frame. Minor tangles: the legs cross at 1.50–1.58, 3.83–3.88 and 23.83–23.92. The head reads more cat than dog at 26–31.

**Language: no, and this is the deciding failure.**
- The dog is a 3D figure of 165 ray-cast spheres with steady shading, posed and walked like a rig. The found images are a faint, duotone-tinted surface texture over it.
- That is the brief's cohort-2 lesson: "a generic 3D maquette with a style laid over it".
- The swarm's own tools (substitution, the cutting score, imagery classes) never touch the dog's shape or motion.
- On the calls the 2-frame swaps read as a shimmer of skin pattern with no recognisable image. On the soft passages the coat looks like plain beige plastic.
- It is different from 04 and the collage swarms only because it is a 3D render, not because of its montage.

**Craft:** the coat images are illegible at full frame, and the maquette dominates. The second "it's" is about 0.18 s late (10.58 against 10.40). The end card's "whole images … sheep, lion, horse" is not visible.

**Sound:** voice onsets are at or before each reaction. Calls about −17 dB, soft voice −26 to −29 dB, room about −47 dB. **Rights:** clean; the 39 sources are byte-identical to the kit, and 42 entries match found-frames.json.

**Repair conditions (ranked)**
1. The dog's visible body is made of the substituted images themselves: each silhouette is formed by a cut image, with no steady 3D shading underneath. Freeze any frame and the dog still reads as one dog made of image fragments.
2. On the calls, at least one substituted image per second is identifiable at full frame as its source (cat, tiger, horse …).
3. On the soft voice, the substitutions stay visible as images, not plain beige (8.3–8.7, 14.6, 24–24.6).
4. Lying across the sill (24.3–31.9), the hind legs fold under the body, and the head reads as a dog's, not a cat's.
5. The second "it's" appears by 10.45.

All six choices read: backing away (reverse playback, body facing the call); turned back (turns 4.0–4.4, lies head away 6.5); turning to look (7.7, within tolerance, no double exposure, but a hard cut from lying to standing); coming head low (strongest passage, nose to the ground 14.8–23.6); lying across the sill (flat from 24.2, front half inside the jamb, lit warm); looking in and staying (head lifts 26.6–28.0, body unmoved; weak in that the door leaf fills most of the doorway).

Bottom-up: every figure a SAM-masked cut from public-domain footage; live provenance strip with source, year, rights, frame, operation; "COMPOSED SCENE · not a recorded event" throughout and composed poses tagged; unknown timecodes left unknown; every source credited.

Round-1 conditions: 1–4 met; 5 mostly met (operation tags "turn" and "comes round" still partly name choices).

Remaining defects (polish, not pursued): mask frame f046 renders legless (1.8, 20.5, 22.0); hard cut at the turn 7.5; the door leaf blocks the lit interior during the look-in; small strip text (~9–10 px).

Verdict: PASS. Stopped here per the SCION doctrine (stop at an independent pass).

method · every asset and its rights · ledger of attempts · build · the prompt

SCION 54Archive-to-puppet lineage · 38 s · PASS

Provenance graph

The film can answer, for any pixel, where it came from and what was done to it.

Made of

One frame of the home-movie dog cut along its SAM mask and recorded cut lines into head, body and legs, the tail from another frame, each in a dusk and a lamplight colouring; the dog's own turning frames for both turns. The Straight Talk door, a woman cut from Straight Talk, the hand from The Door to Heaven, a miner's lantern from Anthracite, wall stone from the home movie, Quick, Draw! moon, trees and fence. Every image is a node in a 367-node lineage graph with a content hash, its parents, its operation and its rights.

Moved by

Pivots and a planted-foot walk for the puppet; hard substitution through single clean archive frames for the turns; the door's closing swing played as its opening. Every frame is composed only from its recorded layers: 287 derived images re-run from source reproduce their hashes, and frames rebuilt from the records alone match the render bit for bit.

The sheets

Did the description survive?

Measured from this swarm's own render (its trace) by check_handmade.py. The score's time, then the time the choice appears.

backs away 1.00 → 1.00 swatches 2.33 → 2.33 sturns its back 3.42 → 3.42 swatches 7.46 → 7.46 scomes, head low 14.71 → 14.71 slies across the sill 23.79 → 23.79 s
  • Never inside. Holds: closest 0.59 m from the door (the sill is at 0.56); ends 0.59 m.
  • Follows the score. Holds: r = 1.000 between its position and the score's.
  • The refusal shows the back. Holds: turned in 97% of the refusal's frames.

The critics

Each round is a fresh agent that sees the film, the brief and this swarm's SCION prompt, never the builder's notes, and returns a verdict and falsifiable repairs. Stopped at an independent pass.

Critic round 1 · DOES NOT PASS (narrowly)
# SCION 54 · critic round 1 · DOES NOT PASS (narrowly)

Independent critic (fresh agent; frames every 0.5 s plus dense windows, contact sheets, full resolution; verdict from the picture before any builder notes).

Per choice: backing away reads (head below the back at 1.05, moves away facing the door, legs alternating); turned back weak (the turn uses archive frames of the dog walking toward camera; 3.84–7.42 the "back" is the cut body with its head cut away, a headless furry lump; double-exposure tangles at 3.50 and 3.75–3.80); turning to look reads (the head pops round to the door at 7.466, on time; a two-headed crossfade at 7.71 and a blur at 7.92); coming head low reads; lying across the sill reads (flat by 24.2, head and forelegs inside the lit frame, rump and tail out); looking in weak to reads (the head lifts toward the doorway at 26.6–26.8, a little late, small). Not a dog in every frame. The door leaf is open from 0 s, so "the door opens" reads only as light.

Bottom-up: yes. The audit holds (367 nodes, 287 derivatives re-executed with no mismatch, no orphan layers, 9 frames rebuilt, documented and inferred tags kept apart); the dog is a puppet of cuts from one archive frame swapped with real archive turning frames, re-cut and re-timed to the score. Fundamentally different from PAE-00 and from simply re-performing footage. The on-screen provenance strip never names the dog's state but is barely legible.

Defects: the headless refusal held 3.6 s; crossfade tangles at 3.50, 3.75, 7.71, 7.92; the same head-low pose for fear and approach; the door open from the start; the strip illegible; the soft voice only ~6 dB over room tone. Rights check out (hashes spot-checked against the kit; Quick, Draw! key_ids credited).

Repair conditions (ranked):
1. 3.9–7.42: every frame shows the head as part of the silhouette, turned away or low on the far side, rump and tail toward the door.
2. No frame 3.40–4.0 or 7.46–8.1 shows two overlapping heads or a blurred double body (check 3.50, 3.75, 7.71, 7.92).
3. The head turn into the doorway completes by 26.65.
4. The door leaf visibly moves open between ~6.0 and 7.0.
5. The soft voice ≥10 dB above room tone and ≥6 dB below the calls.

Keep: the retreat, the head-low approach, the lying pose across the sill, the archive hand's low offer, the lineage graph on the end card.
Critic round 2 · PASS
# SCION 54 · critic round 2 · PASS

Fresh independent critic (all 768 scene frames, every frame in the requested windows, contact sheets; judged before reading round 1).

All six choices read on time: backing away (head below the back, moves away facing the door, legs alternating); turned back (archive turning frames 3.42–3.75, one body each frame; profile facing away 3.79–7.42 with the head part of the silhouette, low on the far side, rump toward the door); turning to look (the head comes round to the door at 7.46; a one-frame stutter at 7.67); coming head low (below the back to 23.5, legs stepping); lying across the sill (flat by 24.2, head and forelegs inside the lit frame, rump and tail out); looking in and staying (head level into the lit doorway 26.5–28.4, close to the person's hand). A dog in every frame inspected.

Bottom-up: a puppet of cuts from the archive home movie swapped with its own turning frames; the per-frame source strip legible and never naming the dog's state; the end card's graph built from the 367-node lineage; every audit check passes (287 derivatives re-executed with no mismatch, no orphan layers, 9 frames rebuilt, no unknown rights).

Round-1 conditions: all five met (the door swings open 6.29–6.9 with the light following; soft voice ~12.7 dB above room tone and ~16 dB under the calls).

Remaining (optional, not pursued: stopped at an independent pass per the SCION doctrine): the settled pose 28.6–32 is a lumpy heap whose head sinks below the sill line (the last image of the scene); the 7.67 one-frame reversal; mask-lumpy frames at 3.50 and 7.92; fear and approach share a head-low pose; the dog stands rather than lies through the refusal.

method · every asset and its rights · ledger of attempts · build · the prompt

What comes after

  • The other 48. Each swarm's lens in PAE-01 is its sketch for PAE-02; these six decide what the brief needs before it scales.
  • After the threshold. The next evening, when the door is already open: a swarm that kept the invariants carries its dog one scene further with the same materials. That tests whether its world is a world, or only a look.
  • The language. The score is the first piece of PAELEO the language: a description precise enough to operate and loose enough to be built six ways. Its next form is a file a person writes rather than a machine run.
PAELEO · a research program of CRASH LAB · Bolas · Watson Hartsoe, with Claude. Augmented Environments Lab is the name of the Georgia Tech laboratory and its lineage; PAELEO is a proposed program and language that develops from that tradition. It is not an official name of the lab. SCION prompts are new operational prompts inspired by documented work; they are not claims that the people named wrote or endorsed them.
